# Dema Testing and Quality Matrix

This repo uses native Node.js tests and CLI smoke checks. The goal is behavior coverage: every public safety boundary should have a replayable local check.

## Commands

Run the full local gate:

```bash
npm test
npm run coverage
npm run check
npm run llm:guidance
npm run release:readiness
npm run gtm:readiness
npm run urp:discovery
npm run proof:room
npm run pre-push:seal
npm run layer-a5:prep
git diff --check
```

Run one test file:

```bash
node --test tests/status.test.js
```

Run one test name pattern:

```bash
node --test --test-name-pattern="bounded diagnostic" tests/status.test.js
```

| `tests/kernel-purity-check.test.js`                     | KERNEL-PURITY-GATE-1A read-only effect-boundary gate (`scripts/review/kernel-purity-check.mjs` + `checkKernelPurity`): scans `packages/core/src/*.js` (excluding `*.test.js`) for forbidden side-effect surfaces (`node:fs`/`net`/`http`/`https`/`child_process` + global `fetch`) against an explicit `IO_TIER_ALLOWLIST`. 21 tests — fixture RED/GREEN (each module form: `from`-import, bare side-effect import, `require()`, dynamic `import()`, global `fetch(`), allowlist exemption, `*.test.js` exclusion, comment-line skip, bounded long-line scan, frozen read-only report that writes nothing, stale-allowlist surfacing; real-tree scan classifies `system-snapshot.js` as allowlisted I/O and asserts real-tree `ok:true` (14-entry allowlist finalized per acceptance #8) plus regression guards that prose `fetch (` is not a call, that multi-line imports are caught, and that namespaced `globalThis`/`window`/`global`.`fetch(` are caught; CLI `--json` + human output exit-1-on-violation, exit-0-on-clean, stale-allowlist warning. Wired into `npm run check` (acceptance #8 satisfied: 14-entry allowlist = `system-snapshot.js` + 13 core I/O modules; `pat-research-companion.js` was a `fetch (` prose false positive, fixed by requiring no space before the call paren).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

| `scripts/urp-stage3-closeout.mjs`                       | URP-3.1D Stage 3 Local Index Closeout drift-guard probe (`scripts/urp-stage3-closeout.mjs` · registered in `scripts/check.mjs`): runs the real cryptographic chain (`authorship key init` with `GENERATE AUTHORSHIP KEY` consent → `authorship sign` with `SIGN AUTHORSHIP RECEIPT` consent → `proof passport --json` → `urp index --passport <p> --json` → `urp list --json` → `urp verify <index> --json`) inside a throwaway `mkdtemp` `DEMA_HOME`; emits `bizra.dema.urp_stage3_closeout_demo.v0.1` envelope on stdout with `demo_passed:true` + `truth_label:URP_STAGE_3_LOCAL_INDEX_DEMO_VERIFIED` on success (exit 0); failure envelope to stderr with `truth_label:URP_STAGE_3_LOCAL_INDEX_DEMO_FAILED` (exit 1). Each subprocess wall-time bounded by `URP_STAGE3_CLOSEOUT_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 30000). Tmpdir cleaned in finally block. No persistent closeout receipt. No share/PoI/mint/federation/network/economic fields. Probe wired into `npm run check` so any sub-CLI regression fails the canonical check loudly.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| `tests/envelope-schema-validator.test.js`               | Envelope Schema Validator v0.1 (`packages/core/src/envelope-schema-validator.js` + `packages/core/schemas/*.v0.1.json`) — stdlib-only structural validator for Dema envelope shapes. Supports a JSON-Schema-2020-12 subset: `const`, `enum`, `type` (incl. union types and `integer`), `required`, `properties`, `items`, `pattern`. `validateEnvelope(envelope, schemaDef)` is pure; `validateAgainstRegistry(envelope, {registry?})` routes by the envelope's declared `schema` field against an internal private known-schemas map (auto-loaded at module init from `packages/core/schemas/`). The map is **not** exported as a mutable Map; the registry is reached only via the immutable accessors `KNOWN_SCHEMA_IDS` (frozen array snapshot), `getKnownSchema(id)`, `hasKnownSchema(id)`. Result envelope `bizra.dema.envelope_schema_validator.v0.1` carries `recognized`, `ok`, `truth_label` (`MEASURED` · `VALIDATION_FAILED` · `SCHEMA_UNKNOWN`), `errors[]` with `path`/`code`/`message`, and the standard 6-key boundary stamp. Subset supported: `const` · `enum` · `type` (incl. union types and `integer`) · `required` · `properties` · `items` · `minItems` · `maxItems` · `pattern`. Error codes: `missing_required` · `wrong_type` · `const_mismatch` · `enum_mismatch` · `pattern_mismatch` · `invalid_pattern` (malformed regex in a schema is surfaced structurally; the validator does not throw) · `array_too_short` · `array_too_long`. v0.1 covers three envelope shapes: `bizra.dema.onboarding_seal.v0.1` · `bizra.dema.artifact_safety_eval.v0.1` · `bizra.dema.proof_room_bundle.v0.1`. Wired into the Layer 1 pipeline via `artifact-safety-eval.scanSchema()` (see `tests/artifact-safety-eval-schema-wiring.test.js`). 26 tests covering schema id + registry-accessor immutability + private-registry-not-exported + const/enum/type/pattern/required/items checks + union types + nested arrays + malformed regex (`invalid_pattern`) + `minItems`/`maxItems` bounds + registry routing + SCHEMA_UNKNOWN + injected registry + deep-frozen result + boundary stamp + real onboarding seal + real artifact safety envelope round-trip + structural breakage detection.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

draft with operator intent preserved verbatim, normalized scope (first 8 words), authored consent phrase, valid flag (true when intent + allowed + blocked all non-empty), receipt_shape_ready flag for | `tests/agent-kernel.test.js`                            | C3 · Agent Loop Kernel (`packages/core/src/agent-kernel.js`) — pure state machine per ADR-008 §C3. 8-state machine (INIT → PERCEIVE → PROPOSE → CONSENT_REQUEST → ACT_OR_HOLD → OBSERVE → DECIDE_NEXT → COMPLETE or HALTED). Pure-function `tick(kernel, input)` returns `{kernel, event}` · no I/O inside kernel · LLM call and tool call injected as dependencies by callers (C4 PAT × 7). Halt-gate at every transition · halt input forces HALTED from any state. Iteration cap (default 100) prevents runaway loops. Per-agent memory file path declared as `~/.dema/agents/<id>/memory.json` but not read/written inside kernel (deferred to C11 file access). Each transition emits `bizra.dema.agent_kernel_transition.v0.1` event with `receipt_shape_ready=true` flag for C12 chain advance and a frozen `transition_contract` that declares explicit/bounded/receipt-backed/rare-circuit-tested/human-consent-aware/Ihsan-aligned/CI-enforced posture without minting or advancing the chain inside the kernel. 39 tests covering: kernel structure (5) · state machine transitions (8 happy path) · loop + determinism (3) · halt gates (4) · refusals (4) · adversarial (6, including malformed-kernel canonical transition fields) · event schema (6, including assurance contract, consent decision recording, and refused-transition contract) · valid-transition helper + summary + exports (3). 10 declared blocked_effects (auto_advance · skip_consent · skip_observe · infinite_loop · side_channel_mutation · caller_state_modification · chain_advance_without_complete · receipt_mint_inside_kernel · federation_invocation · node1_or_node2_connection).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Conflict resolution: any effect in BOTH allowed and blocked → blocked wins (refusal default). `buildToolRegistry()` emits `bizra.dema.tool_registry.v0.1` with bind-time verification: rejects entries with missing cap, missing invoke, invalid cap, or name mismatch. `invokeWithEffectCap()` is the gate: looks up tool, verifies cap valid, exact-match consent phrase (no fuzzy, no prefix), invokes with sandboxed args, emits `bizra.dema.effect_cap_invocation.v0.1` event with `receipt_shape_ready` flag for future C12 minting. 30 tests covering: descriptor (6) · adversarial input filtering (5) · registry (5) · invocation gate (8) · invocation event schema (3) · exports + constants (3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

|`tests/event-log.test.js` | OBS-1A observability event-log kernel (`packages/core/src/event-log.js`). 19 tests. Schema `bizra.dema.event*log_entry.v0.1`. `buildEvent()` -> frozen, content-addressed (`event_id = sha256(stableStringify(body))`) record; redacted BY CONSTRUCTION — accepts a command name, outcome enum (`ok`/`error`/`refused`), correlation*id, boundary map, and a PRIMITIVES-ONLY metadata map (rejects arrays/objects/oversized strings so raw argv/content can never enter); fail-closed on missing/invalid fields and invalid `recorded_at_iso`. `appendEvent()`-> re-verifies the event id before writing, appends one JSON line to`$DEMA_HOME/events/log.jsonl`, creates event paths without group/world permissions, and chains `prev_hash`to the prior event id (genesis null) for tamper evidence; local fs only; single-writer append path (callers must serialize concurrent writers).`readEvents()`-> validates explicit`home`, read-only replay that re-derives each `event_id`(content integrity ->`verified`) and verifies the `prev_hash` chain (`chain_intact`); skips + flags corrupt lines without throwing; empty/absent log is vacuously valid. Covers deterministic event*id, redaction rejection, genesis + chained append, round-trip ordering, content-tamper -> `verified:false`, chain-tamper -> `chain_intact:false`, corrupt-line detection, mode checks, tampered event-id rejection, required-home validation. Reader: `scripts/events.mjs`(read-only, exits 1 on tamper or corrupt lines, rejects non-positive`--limit`). Local-only, no auto-hook, no network, no keys, no mint. |

Schema `bizra.dema.recovery_manifest.v0.1`. `buildRecoveryManifest({home})`-> frozen manifest: every file under`DEMA_HOME`content-addressed (SHA-256), entries sorted by`rel_path`(POSIX),`root_hash = sha256(stableStringify(entries))` binding the whole set; deterministic; bounded walk (`MAX_FILES=20000`/`MAX_DEPTH=24`, fail-closed); fail-closed on missing home. `verifyAgainstManifest({home, manifest})`-> re-derives current hashes and reports`verified`+`root_hash_match`+`mismatched`/`missing`/`extra`sets (tamper, incomplete restore, and unexpected files all detected). Covers deterministic root_hash, untouched->VERIFIED, tampered file->mismatched+root MISMATCH, missing file->missing, extra file->extra, fail-closed on absent home. Runnable:`scripts/dema-recovery.mjs backup [--out file] / verify <manifest.json>`(exit 1 on tamper); runbook`docs/RECOVERY.md`. Manifest must be stored OUTSIDE `DEMA_HOME`. Local-only, no network, no keys, no consent, no mint. |

Mutual exclusion enforced across `--registry-stdin` / `--registry-file` / `--use-local-registry` (any combination of two or more → non-zero exit with `mutually exclusive` stderr). Test fixtures use `mkdtemp` ephemeral `DEMA_HOME` + `writeFile` registry stubs; no real model names committed. Operator entry `operator-test-dema-face` (test-only) routes synthesis. | `tests/model-broker-receipt-save-cli.test.js` | Local Model Broker Receipt **SAVE** Preview v0.2 (`dema model-broker route ... --save-receipt --consent "<phrase>"` using `packages/receipts/src/route-receipt-save.js`). **Preview-grade local persistence, NOT canonical chain-bound mint** — `"mint"` is reserved for the C12 chain-bound flow per ADR-008. Exact-string consent enforced: `--consent "GO: save local model route receipt"` (mismatch / missing → non-zero exit + stderr names required phrase). On success: route receipt JSON is **also** written to `$DEMA_HOME/receipts/route-<sha256-hex>.json` (content-addressed filename derived from sha256 of the exact bytes written). **Atomic write** via `writeFile(tmp, content, {flag: "wx"})` → `rename(tmp, final)` so a partial write never appears on disk; temp file is best-effort `unlink`-ed on error. Path safety: `realpath` containment check pre-empts symlink-escape attacks (modeled on receipt-store v0.1.2 PR #64 precedent). **stdout unchanged** when saving (same `bizra.dema.local_model_route_receipt.v0.1` JSON; on-disk file matches stdout byte-for-byte); stderr emits a single `saved receipt to: <path>` info line. Default behavior unchanged when `--save-receipt` is absent (no file written, `receipts/` not touched). Boundary on saved receipt: all 8 effect flags false (runtime · model_invocation · network_used · federation · mint · token_economy · urp_networking · prompt_invocation_allowed). Two consecutive runs produce two distinct files because the route receipt's `timestamp` field differs between runs (sha256 content-addressing reflects actual content). No model invocation. No network. No `~/.dema/receipts/` mutation outside the explicit save path. |
| `tests/routed-llm-invocation.test.js` | Routed LLM Invocation Bridge v0.1 (`packages/core/src/routed-llm-invocation.js`): in-process tests with `fetchImpl` mock — does NOT depend on a running Ollama. Thin bridge that takes a route receipt + prompt + invoke-consent and calls `packages/core/src/llm-adapter.js`'s `invokeLocalLLM` with `selected_model_id` as the model name. Reuses ALL adapter safety gates (localhost-only, model whitelist, prompt length bound, exact consent phrase) — bridge does not duplicate. Envelope schema `bizra.dema.local_model_routed_invocation_result.v0.1` wraps the original route receipt + the adapter's invocation result + a derived 9-key boundary (`runtime` + `model_invocation` + `network_used` + `localhost_only=true` + `remote_provider=false` + `federation=false` + `mint=false` + `token_economy=false` + `urp_networking=false`). Boundary is derived: `model_invocation=true` only when adapter returns `invocation_status=completed`; `network_used=true` only when the adapter actually attempted the HTTP fetch (pre-fetch failures like `prompt_empty`, `consent_phrase_mismatch`, `model_not_in_whitelist`, `endpoint_not_localhost`, `prompt_too_long`, `fetch_not_available` leave `network_used=false`). Tests: successful routed invocation (whitelisted model + mocked localhost response → completed envelope); placeholder/non-whitelisted model fails at adapter whitelist gate; empty prompt; oversized prompt; consent mismatch; timeout (network attempted, model not invoked); no `selected_model_id` short-circuits before adapter (envelope `invocation_result=null` + warning); deep-frozen envelope + 9-key boundary keys verified. |
| `tests/model-broker-invoke-cli.test.js` | Model Broker `--invoke` CLI Preview v0.1 (`dema model-broker route ... --invoke --prompt "<text>" --invoke-consent "<phrase>" [--timeout-ms N]`): subprocess tests for the routed invocation CLI surface. **All tests exit non-zero BEFORE any real HTTP attempt** — does NOT depend on a running Ollama (successful-invocation tests live in `routed-llm-invocation.test.js` with `fetchImpl` mock). **Hard ordering enforced**: `--invoke` requires `--save-receipt` (route durability before invocation); `--invoke` requires `--prompt` and `--invoke-consent`; route saves FIRST, then invokes, then envelope to stdout. Per-model consent format reuses existing adapter pattern: `"GO: invoke local LLM at <selected_model_id>"`. Deterministic + idempotent: same inputs twice → same`receipt_id`/path, one file. Receipt records schema·claim·claim_state·assumption_envelope·assumption_gate_result; no key/token/federation material. No CLI (1C-bis/1D). No PoI/economy/XP. |

| `tests/flywheel-convergence-attestation.test.js` | CONVERGENCE-ATTEST-1A sign + ground the convergence verdict (`packages/flywheel/src/flywheel-convergence-attestation.js`). Caps the proof spine (verify → ATTEST → seal): turns FLYWHEEL-REPLAY-1B's ephemeral verdict into a signed, content-addressed, **Level-B GROUNDED** attestation — the artifact Block0 §18 needs to seal "one full flywheel run". `attestConvergence({demaHome, operatorPubkeyPem, now})`runs the convergence verifier and, only if convergent, signs an attestation committing to the chain root,`chain_length`, `task_count`, a `task_fingerprint`(sha256 of the verified tasks), and the four`layers`. Pure-with-key-load (signs the verdict like SAT-VALIDATE-1A; no consent phrase, no disk write). `verifyConvergenceAttestation({attestation, demaHome, pubkeyPem})`is two-tier: **Level-A** re-derives`attestation_id`+ verifies the Ed25519 signature under the EXTERNAL pubkey; **Level-B** re-runs`verifyConvergentTaskChain` against the LIVE chain and confirms the attested verdict still re-derives (chain root, length, task fingerprint). Schema:`bizra.dema.convergence_attestation.v0.1`; rule:`flywheel_task_convergence.v0.1`. 6 tests. THE GROUNDING CATCH: tampering a bound entry AFTER attesting leaves the signature valid (`level_a_signature_valid:true`) but fails Level-B (`stage:"grounding", reason:"live_chain_not_convergent"`) — signed ≠ true, grounded = true. Fail-closed: a non-convergent chain cannot be attested (`not_convergent`); missing `now`→`created_at_iso_required`; local key ≠ `operatorPubkeyPem`→`operator_key_mismatch`; foreign-key verify → `signature_invalid`. Level-B grounding is TOTAL — every signed field re-derives from the live convergence result (`verifier_rule_id`, `convergence_schema`, `chain_length`, `canonical_chain_root`(from the verifier's single loaded snapshot),`task_count`, `layers`, `task_fingerprint`); a re-signed attestation with a wrong `task_count` passes Level-A but fails Level-B (`task_count_mismatch`). A malformed external pubkey fails closed (no crash) and a rotated local key (≠ `operatorPubkeyPem`) refuses to attest (`operator_key_mismatch`). No private-key/public-economy material. |

## Harness-only probes

| Probe                             | Surface covered                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `scripts/urp-stage4-closeout.mjs` | URP-4.1D Stage 4 Choose closeout drift-guard probe (`scripts/urp-stage4-closeout.mjs` registered in `scripts/check.mjs`): runs the real local proof/choose chain in a throwaway `mkdtemp` `DEMA_HOME` (`authorship key init` with `GENERATE AUTHORSHIP KEY` consent -> `authorship sign` with `SIGN AUTHORSHIP RECEIPT` consent -> `proof passport --json` -> `urp index --passport <p> --json` -> `urp verify <index> --json` -> `urp choose <index> --decision MARK_SHAREABLE --consent "MARK URP ENTRY SHAREABLE" --json` -> `urp choose <index> --decision MARK_LOCAL_ONLY --consent "MARK URP ENTRY LOCAL-ONLY" --json` -> `urp choose list --json` -> `urp choose verify <receipt> --json` for both generated choose receipts). Emits `bizra.dema.urp_stage4_closeout_demo.v0.1` with `demo_passed:true` and `truth_label:URP_STAGE_4_CHOOSE_CLOSEOUT_VERIFIED` on success. Failure emits `URP_STAGE_4_CHOOSE_CLOSEOUT_FAILED` and exits 1. Per-subprocess timeout: `URP_STAGE4_CLOSEOUT_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 30000). Cleans tmpdir in `finally`. No new CLI surface, no operator `DEMA_HOME` mutation, no network, no share publication, no PoI, no mint, no federation, no economic claim, and no persistent closeout receipt. |

## Quality expectations

Every new public surface should add or update tests for:

1. schema tag,
2. human output,
3. JSON output when available,
4. safe default,
5. non-execution boundary,
6. hostile or malformed input,
7. deterministic output when relevant.

## Coverage threshold

`npm run coverage` uses Node's native test coverage gate with enforced
thresholds:

```text
lines: 95
branches: 85
functions: 95
```

The primary GitHub Actions check and BIZRA Review Gate run this coverage command
after `npm test` and before `npm run check`.

## Secret-scanning gate (CI)

`.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml` runs gitleaks v8.30.1 on every pull request
and every push to main. Invariants:

- SHA-256-verified binary install (no marketplace-action license dependency)
- SHA-pinned `actions/checkout` reference (matches 100% SHA-pinning canon)
- `gitleaks detect --source . --no-banner --verbose --exit-code 1 --redact`
- Fails the workflow on any finding (`--exit-code 1`)
- Redacts secrets from CI logs (`--redact`) so the workflow output itself
  never becomes the leak surface
- Boundary: read-only audit · no repo write · no external publish

The gate is independent of `check.yml` and `bizra-review.yml` — a secret
finding fails its own workflow without blocking unrelated CI runs.
